## 5. THE RETURN CONFESSION — THE SPEC (s20–s21; Romance Writer, Opus; the Knowledge Auditor, the Voice Editor and the Standards & Spoiler Guard all sign it; sound Catholic theology throughout)

### 5.0 Rules
1. **POV Jack.** Osei's inner life is never narrated (his hand on his knee, his glasses, his laugh — what Jack sees). Jack's interiority allowed, one sentence of thought then the next thing said.
2. **It is not a confession for most of it** (brief): nothing Jack is distraught about is a sin. **The narration never implies it is.** Ending a relationship honestly is not a sin; loving two people is not a sin; having planned a future is not a sin; the near-kiss that stopped is not a mortal sin (it was temptation, resisted; at most an imprudence — and only if Jack raises it).
3. **Osei will not tell him what to do** (brief). He asks questions. He does not approve or disapprove the plan; he does not say "you're doing the right thing"; he does not weigh Lena against Skye. Talking it out loud helps — the scene shows it helping (Jack's breathing, the order of his sentences, the moment he hears himself).
4. **The seal, both ways (🔒 absolute):** Osei holds Lena's confession (April 2009; and today's, an hour ago) and Theo's (July 2009). **Nothing he says, asks, or reacts to may draw on either** — he asks nothing about Lena that a stranger couldn't ask; his face does nothing when Jack says her name. **The Knowledge Auditor signs every Osei sentence.** Nothing Jack says here ever reaches anyone in the episode.
5. **Skye is honored** (brief, "After S2E11"): Osei blessed her in the Anacapa lot at a quarter to five on Feb 5 ("Then stand with them."); he says so once, tenderly. She is never "almost the thing," never a cautionary tale, never the lesser love — **"a real love given up for the one that is also his vocation"** (brief, the thematic key) — **and that is never said as a thesis by anyone.** Jack's words fumble; that's the point.
6. **Physician's metaphors, Ghanaian English, unhurried, proverbial, mischievous** (bible §8.5 Voice); one Twi word at most ("Ɛyɛ" — it is well — at the end is natural).

### 5.1 Staging (s20 → s21)
- 4:44: the last penitent comes out; the light over the door stays on; Osei opens the door and looks down the empty aisle at the one man left in the back pew, and reads him (a physician's look) — and says, **"Come."** Not the box: **his office off the sacristy hall** (bible §8.5/S1E21: two chairs **side by side, facing the crucifix** — never pointed out; the staging is the "same direction"); the turntable with the highlife on (the music field) — he lifts the needle as they come in; rubber boots by the door; reading glasses on their cord; the box of tissues that lives on the desk for exactly this; [optional, Spoiler Guard] an airmail envelope to London on the desk, addressed, no name visible (🔒 Kwame).
- He puts on the purple stole from the back of the chair — "in case" — and sits in the other chair, and looks at the crucifix, not at Jack. **Face-to-face confession in the office is canonical** (the penitent may choose the screen or not; Jack chooses this by sitting down).

### 5.2 Beats, in order (the Writer orders lines within each; never reorders across)
1. **"Bless me, Father" — and then he can't go on with the formula**, because there's nothing after it that belongs there yet. Osei: "Then don't. Tell me what happened. We'll see what it is after." (or his better line)
2. **The plan, plain:** both of them free by the end of the week; she ends it Sunday, after Mass, to his face; Jack tells Skye Tuesday, when she's back, to her face — he promised Skye that, in a darkroom, before he left. **Nothing more of the levee than Jack would tell a priest** (he may say they nearly kissed and both stopped; he may say they told each other everything since October; he does not recite it).
3. **Osei's first question**, not advice (e.g. "And what is it you want me to do with that?"). Jack: nothing. He isn't asking permission. He knows.
4. **Why he's distraught:** **Skye.** He loves her (Osei: "I know. I have heard you say so, in that box." — S2E11, verbatim-adjacent) and **he had honestly planned to marry her someday** — not soon; someday; the chair at Nonna's table; **Nonna's text, "Maria Caterina is a good name for a wife"** — and that he **told Skye** what Nonna said, and she heard it (`skye_knows_wife_line` ✓). (This is the choice `s2e17_osei`, §5.6.) 🔒 **The widower hears "a good name for a wife":** Osei is quiet a long time; his hand rests on the bad knee; he takes his glasses off and puts them on again. **Nothing glossed. Ever.**
5. **"Same direction":** Jack tries to say why, and can't, and then says it with somebody else's words — **Saint-Exupéry, from the cross-stitch over the phone in his mother's kitchen on Glenwood Avenue** (a wedding present; he grew up reading it while he waited for the phone to ring): *Love does not consist in gazing at each other, but in looking outward together in the same direction.* Then his own words, fumbling (Writer's): Skye looks at him — the best thing that ever looked at him; she went to Mass to understand him, she knelt, she walked at the March — and **Lena looks where he's looking**. It doesn't make Skye less. It makes it worse. **Osei (a pilot crashed in the Sahara wrote it — he knew something about direction)** may say one physician's or pilot's sentence; he does not endorse a choice.
6. **"To what question?"** — Osei's own January line (S2E08, ungated, in Previously): Jack answers it now, in his words — something like *the question was which way I'm facing* — and is not sure it's the right answer, and says so. Osei: no verdict. ("Then you have one more question than you had in January. That is progress, in my old profession.")
7. **The wish, named:** [`s2e11_sorry=="candor"` ✓] Osei: **"On the morning you left, I told you the part of you that wished she had not stopped was not sorrow yet — to bring it to me when you could name it."** Jack names what's there now — not that wish anymore; this one: that it could have been both, that he didn't have to hurt her to tell the truth. **Osei: that has a name. It is grief. Grief is not a sin. I cannot absolve it; you carry it, and He carries it with you.** (Sound: the Church absolves sins, not sorrows.) [else: Osei asks him to name what he's carrying, and the same answer follows.]
8. **Skye, honored:** Osei says once that he blessed her in the lot ("I put water in her hair at a quarter to five in the morning. She shut her eyes. I remember that."). [`s2e8_noon=="candor"` ✓] He does not say "I told you so" about "if you make her the answer you make her the occasion" — if he refers to it at all, it's to say she was never only an occasion; she was a person, and Jack loved her as one, and that is why this costs. (Brief standing: "love wills the good of the other" — the real good in it.)
9. **The small laugh (brief, binding):** **"Jack. You have been in that chair twenty minutes, and you have not confessed one thing."** — Osei laughs, small, from the belly (bible: "laughs from the belly"); and Jack, surprised, laughs too, once (#101 vary; a laugh "into" nothing).
10. **The venial sins of New Orleans (no mortal sin exists to confess — `confession_owed` 0):** Jack examines himself out loud and finds the small things, honestly; **fixed list** (all paths), the Writer's words: (a) **Clovis's bourbon at one in the morning on Ash Wednesday — "so I wouldn't want a cigarette; on the first hour of Lent"** (intemperance, not drunkenness); (b) **snapping at Theo on Thursday** for nothing (anger, unkindness); (c) **fasting Fridays partly to beat her** (pride; Osei: "Fasting out of pride is still fasting. The pride you can leave with me."); (d) **an uncharitable hope about Julian — that he'd be unkind to her on Sunday, so Jack wouldn't have to go on admiring him** (the Writer may find the truer shape; it must be small, real and venial); then **`s2e17_last`** (§5.6) adds one more by option.
11. **Devotional confession, in Osei's voice (brief: explained; CCC 1458's sense, never cited by number):** **you were not required to bring any of this; the Church only requires mortal sins; but she is glad when you bring the small ones** — a doctor likes to see the healthy patient too; it is how he learns what healthy looks like; small cuts bleed a long time if nobody looks at them; it forms the conscience ("it teaches your conscience its own handwriting"), it fights the habits while they're small, it lets Christ heal what you'd have called nothing. **Short.** Two or three sentences; never a lecture; one physician's line.
12. **Contrition** (Jack's own words or the Act of Contrition — he says the Act from memory, the Baltimore wording or the one Ma taught him; Writer's call) · **Absolution, exactly** (Rite of Penance, ICEL 1974): *"God, the Father of mercies, through the death and resurrection of his Son has reconciled the world to himself and sent the Holy Spirit among us for the forgiveness of sins; through the ministry of the Church may God give you pardon and peace, and I absolve you from your sins in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit."* — "Amen." (The narration may give it whole or in part; Osei's hand raised over him; no screen.)
13. **The penance:** **an Our Father for Skye, on Tuesday, before he goes up the hill — "Slowly. For her. Not for you."** And then, taking off the stole, as a doctor, not a confessor: "Did you eat today?" — [s19 always] "Gloria made me." — "Then she has done my job. Go and sleep. A man should not tell the truth on four hours' sleep; it comes out crooked." (Or the Writer's better.) **"Ɛyɛ."** (It is well.)
14. **Out:** the needle back on the record; the hall; the vigil bell.

### 5.3 What Osei never does
Tell him what to do; weigh the two women; mention Lena's confession or Theo's or the Point by so much as a pause tied to them; promise Tuesday will go well; joke about Skye; preach Saint-Exupéry; call anything a mortal sin; refuse absolution (there's no ground); say "devotional confession" as a term without explaining it in his own words.

### 5.4 Theology checks (Standards & Spoiler Guard + Voice Editor)
Mortal sin requires grave matter, full knowledge, deliberate consent — none present; venial sins need not be confessed but are encouraged (devotional confession); temptation is not sin; feelings are not sins; ending a non-marital relationship honestly is not a sin; the absolution formula exact; the penitent's freedom to confess face to face; Ash Wednesday fast rules correct; Friday abstinence; nothing implies sacramental grace is earned.

### 5.5 Length and register
≈2,300 words (the episode's longest scene). Restraint is the fire (§4.4): Jack comes apart a little — the withdrawal, four days of no sleep, the tissues on the desk — but under real pressure he gets very quiet and very plain (bible §6 Voice). **One** "I'm good" is allowed only if Osei catches it ("You say 'good' the way my patients said 'fine'.").

### 5.6 The choices
- **`s2e17_osei`** (beat 4; key every option; cue on [Sincerity] "Father Osei heard that."):
  - **[Sincerity]** "I was going to marry her someday, Father. Not soon. Someday. I had the chair picked out at my grandmother's table." → `s2e17_osei:"marry"`, trait.sincerity +1 · key: *In Father Osei's office you told him you'd planned to marry Skye someday — you had the chair picked out at your grandmother's table.*
  - **[Candor]** "My grandmother said Maria Caterina is a good name for a wife. I told Skye she said it. I meant it." → `"wife"`, trait.candor +1, soul.honesty +1 · key: *…you told him what Nonna said — Maria Caterina is a good name for a wife — and that you'd told Skye, and meant it.*
  - **[Restraint]** *Say nothing for a while. Let him wait.* → `"silence"`, trait.control +1 · Osei waits as long as it takes (he has sat up with a great many people at this hour); then Jack says it plainly (the marriage, Nonna's line) · key: *…you sat a long time without saying anything. Then you told him you'd meant to marry her someday.*
- **`s2e17_last`** (beat 10; no key, no cue) — the last small thing:
  - **[Candor]** "And I bought a pack of cigarettes. I haven't opened it." → Osei: **"That is not a sin. That is a very expensive rosary."** (and a physician's half-sentence about keeping it in the pocket and the hand off it)
  - **[Sincerity]** "And I let her stay inside my coat. On Mardi Gras. I wanted her there." → Osei on temptation and sin, soundly and briefly: wanting is not consenting; he stopped; holding someone in a cold coat is not a sin — deceiving is, and he deceived no one; "God does not usually waste a trip."
  - **[Wit]** "And I told a thirteen-year-old 'soon' like I was promising to clean the gutters." → Osei laughs again; "That one I will absolve twice."

## 5a. THE PACK — THE SPEC (s11 → s22; Romance Writer for s11/s13/s18s/s21; every writer carries it)
- **Canon:** a pack of **Marlboro Reds** bought **Thu Feb 18 ≈10:05 a.m. MST** at the El Paso station off I-10 (S2E12 s12's), paid in Gloria's ones; the cellophane never broken; it rides in **the Carhartt's breast pocket**, where the tin rode from Dec 19 to Feb 16 (and the RAZR beside it). `bought_pack_ep`, `pack_unopened` on every path.
- **Why he carries it (the narration may let him think it once, never explain it as a method):** if it's in his pocket he chooses every hour; if it's in a trash can at El Paso he's at the next exit buying another. Skye calls it "the most Method thing I've ever heard" (s13); Osei calls it "a very expensive rosary" ([Candor] s21); Lena refuses to hold it ([Candor] s11).
- **Never:** opened, lit, smelled through a broken seal, handed to anyone who keeps it, counted on `jack_cigs`. **Lena quietly moved** (brief) is shown three ways only (five fingers [`s2e16_tin=="candor"`], the look, the sunflower seeds), never told.
- **Withdrawal, frankly (brief: "withdrawal, itchy hands"):** hour six (s01: hands at the shirt pocket); day two (s10: the itch, the fuse, the fog; Kevin's 48–72 hours; he snaps at Theo); day three (s16: bread and water on top of it; s17: the stations; s18: the night); day four (s19–s21: steadier, raw). Real physical detail; never comic for long; never melodrama.
- 🔒 **Its fate** (room's call; bible #29 note): suggested — at Easter, unopened, into her tin.

## 6. SKYE — FIVE CONTACTS (s01, s08, s13, s18p/s18s, s22; Romance Writer, Opus) — warm as ever; she suspects nothing; honor her fully
- **Purpose:** four days before he tells her, Skye is exactly who she has always been — funny, brilliant, generous, a little "on," devastating when she drops it — and **the player should miss her already and hate what's coming.** Every contact warm and true on his side; **he says nothing of it on the phone** (brief; his promise: to her face). **The ache is that he means every word.** No rung; nothing of her body sent or described.
- **The four-part mix across her contacts (brief "Skye: keep the whole mix"; Voice Editor checks):** *play* (the ashes bit, s08; "Put Ted on." and the goose, s13) · *intellect* (**Matthew 6's "makeup note"; "a costume that isn't allowed to be a performance"** s08; the pack as Method, s13) · *heat* (one low line in her real voice each call, within the standard) · *fun / vulnerability / generosity* ("Six days. I'm counting. I don't count." s08; Tuesday's dinner — she's never cooked for anybody, s13; "I miss you so much it's embarrassing" s13; the count *6 → 5 → 4 → 3*).
- 🔒 **Her secret Lent (bible §10.2, room canon): she has given up the first take — answering as somebody else first.** From Ash Wednesday she answers every call as herself ("Hi."), and plays only after. Jack notices once (s08, one clause — "no voice first; he doesn't know what to make of it") and never again on the page; **never explained here**; revealed at her return talk (S2E19–E20 range): "I gave up doing voices for Lent. Forty days of being only me. And look what it got me." — it must read then as her magnificence, never as irony at her expense.
- **Goodbyes new each contact:** "Bye, Dust." (s08) · "Bye, Pack." (s13) · texts sign off with the count.
- **"Love you":** offered **once**, at Blythe (s13 [Sincerity] `said_love_thu`), where Lena has walked to the store (her choice; she doesn't hear it). Not offered on the Basin call (Lena in the van).
- **Never:** Skye naming Lena; Skye hearing anything of the levee, the tellings, Theo's terror, Laney's thaw; a prescient line ("dont say me," any wink at Tuesday — **her Tuesday plans are innocent and that is the knife**); Skye as a cautionary tale; a bit that sneers at his faith (her ashes bit turns to respect); Jack evasive or cold to her; Jack telling her anything early.

## 6a. THE TELLINGS — RULES (s09 Jack → Theo; s12 Lena → Laney; brief "After S2E16", binding)
1. **Only the breakup is said.** Jack: "I'm ending it with Skye. Tuesday. When she's back." Lena (seen, not heard, from a picnic table): she's ending it with Julian, Sunday. **Nothing further is stated — not Lena, not Jack, not the levee, not why.** Everything is implied (Theo's silence and his "leg cramp"; Laney's hands over her mouth, the boulder, the coconut).
2. **Theo and Laney in their own registers:** Theo goes short and true ("Okay." / "Okay." / "Be good to her." / "Don't tell me anything else."), and lies to Skye later by over-specifying; Laney goes speechless for the first time in recorded history and then pageant-generous in a single gesture ("Hold her. With your *life*.").
3. **The terror of jinxing peaks:** neither asks the next question; **neither tells the other** ("Don't." — "I wasn't —" — "Don't you *dare*."); no knocking wood (#237 retired); nobody says "jinx." Margot sees the contagion (her note); Kevin says keep not saying it.
4. **Jack and Lena know the alliance exists** (they named it on the levee) and watch it knowingly — **never acknowledged to Theo or Laney**, never a wink shared on the page beyond one look across a van roof. **The Easter reveal stays intact.**
5. **Nobody else is told:** Kevin, Margot, every Broussard, Gloria, Ma, Nonna, Skye — nothing. Osei hears it under the seal.
6. **POV:** Jack never hears Lena's words to Laney; he infers only "she told her" — and he knows exactly how much, because he knows what Lena would say and what she wouldn't (one sentence of his inference; no more).

## 10. KNOWLEDGE (the Auditor's checklist)
- **Theo learns (s09):** Jack is ending it with Skye, Tuesday, when she's back. **Nothing else** — not why, not Lena, not the levee. He does not tell Laney.
- **Laney learns (s12):** Lena is ending it with Julian, Sunday. **Nothing else** (Jack infers that much and no more). She does not tell Theo. **She also learns (s07):** Lena gave up Facebook (and Fridays, the Stations). **Jack learns** Laney knows (he sees it) and that Theo and Laney aren't telling each other (he sees that too).
- **Lena learns (on the page):** that Jack will fast Fridays (s07 [Sincerity/Wit]; s16 on every path); that he bought a pack and didn't open it (s11); [Candor s11] that he offered it to her; that he took Skye's calls (she hears his half at the Basin; not at Blythe); nothing of what he told Theo (she may infer from Theo; the narration never says). **She does not learn** the Blythe call's contents, Tuesday's dinner, the porch light, what he told Osei.
- **Skye knows:** his ash picture; Matthew 6 talk; that he's fasting/giving up cigarettes; **the pack** (he told her); [s13 Candor] that he'll "tell her all of it" Tuesday (she hears stories); [s18p Sincerity] that he sat on her porch at 2 a.m.; [s18s Candor] that he sat in a stairwell not smoking. **She knows nothing** of the levee, the tellings, Lena, Theo's terror. Theo's lie at Blythe is unglossed — she doesn't catch it (or the narration doesn't say she does).
- **Osei learns (s21; seal):** the plan; that Jack loves Skye and had meant to marry her; Nonna's line; the "same direction"; the venial sins. **He already holds (🔒 seal):** Lena's April 2009; Lena's confession of an hour ago; Theo's July 2009 — **none of it touches a word he says.**
- **The Broussards:** nothing answered. Tony knows Jack would answer if he could ([market] and that "Tuesday" is when — [Candor s05m]; he doesn't know what Tuesday is). Celeste knows they decided something (her nurse's eye — inference, not knowledge). Jude heard "Tuesday" (the truth to the girl in Canada; nothing more). Josie: [Sincerity s06] "ask me at Easter." Mémère: her own verdicts. Luc: the inch, again.
- **Gloria:** the tin; [market] Tony's card; "church, three-thirty." Nothing else.
- **Kevin / Margot:** what they see — the distance, the bread and water, the pack, Theo and Laney not talking. Margot writes it down. Neither is told anything.
- **Ma / Nonna / Frank:** nothing (Frank: the ashes; Ma: [optional] his voice).
- **Period knowledge:** nobody quotes Vonn, White, Lysacek or Tiger Woods; nobody knows anything after Sat Feb 20; nobody knows what happens Sunday.
